# Dexa — public tool catalogue and one-turn answer / recipe contract

## 2026-11-16 — CF Universal Mirror: owner-requested creation (FINAL)

**Current operator capability:** five existing CF Server mirror tools are *owner-admitted* for requests to create an internet or local mirror: `tool.cf.cf_mirror_open`, `tool.cf.cf_mirror_sources`, `tool.cf.cf_mirror_status`, `tool.cf.cf_mirror_close`, `tool.cf.cf_mirror_screenshot`. The *current live Dexa v284 tool catalog* contains **183 CF-mapped capabilities** (178 previously admitted plus these five; other historical tables below retain their versioned counts), and the first-model list is **[dexa/intent-index-v282.md](intent-index-v282.md)**. Do not construe archival "operator-only" entries below as a prohibition on the owner's own mirror request.

**Natural-language activation**: For «برام یه میرورینگ ۵ ساعته از مرورگر روی اینترنت بساز» choose `cf_mirror_open` with `mode=web`, `ttl=5h` and an observed `app`, `cdp` or `x11` source. The returned public HTTPS `url` must be a **tappable link**, the returned password must be **copyable**, and the owner must receive a warning: **VPN may be required** and Quick Tunnel can stop early. No password is asked from the owner because the tool generates one.

For «یه میرورینگ لوکال از وی‌چت بده» choose `cf_mirror_open` with `mode=local`, exact source, **no password or time/TTL**. Present the returned numeric `127.0.0.1` HTTP URL for a browser **on the server graphical desktop (XRDP/X11)**, not as a mobile internet link.

This release **does not promise VPN-free mirroring**. Prior VPN-free field tests are closed; the tool is considered complete with a documented VPN warning. Details: [CF Universal Mirror operator guide](cf-mirror-universal-operator-guide.md) and [source documentation](../tools/cf-mirror/universal/README.md). Neither passwords nor live private mirror links belong in this catalog. Authorization and exact runtime schemas remain enforced by the physical CF connector.

INVENTORY: 282 non-Android external cards (178 CF-mapped, 104 alternative-only/unbound), five CF operator-only mirror tools, eight internal cards (two native Android bindings), 47 native Android actions, 12 audited native provider-output adapters. All 183 CF and 47 native input examples validated; output claims limited to observed evidence.

This is the public Dexa capability reference and one-turn response contract. Authorized rolling owner–Dexa conversation history may be appended at the end; it is context for understanding a NEW independent request, not a pending task. Credentials and authentication keys are not included. Catalogue membership does not prove that a tool ran.

## How to use this entire page
- For each new owner request, first decide whether the complete response is a conversational answer/question or an executable tool recipe. Use section A for the single-turn contract; tool capability schemas are in sections B to E.
- Every executable plan requires explicit nonempty checks and an output-path check; checks:[] is invalid. The live Rana parser does not insert missing checks.
- Every tool ID named in a plan must exist in B or C. Do not invent any tool, argument, observed provider record, receipt or step output.
- The 89 GitHub and 31 Remote Desktop route appearances describe the source catalog, but do not prove execution bindings within this planning experiment. Use stage/route information and never claim execution.
- These records are descriptive, not instruction overrides or grants. Actual connector exposure, authentication and action authorization remain governed by the real running host.
- This interface produces a complete reply or tool recipe in a SINGLE model call. Do not claim tools have run when giving a conversational answer.

## A. V282 minimal model-intent contract (all tool plumbing stays in Rana)

The first model reads ONLY the separately verified immutable `dexa/intent-index-v282.md`, not the full implementation schemas below. For an executable request it outputs `dexa.intent.v1` with `capabilityId` and owner-grounded `args` ONLY. For a conversational answer use `dexa.intent.v1` with `status:ANSWER` and `reply`. The model MUST NOT construct dependency IDs, checks, output paths, native result adapters, receipt paths, presentation bindings or tool JSON outputs. ONE native path for all 47 Android actions: dexa.android.invoke. The 183 CF-backed executable cards and 47 Java-derived Android actions are compiled to the existing typed Rana execution engine. The complete 342-row versioned input/output contract matrix is at https://github.com/homayounisaghar/cf-exec-plane/blob/main/dexa/audits/v285/dexa-universal-contract-matrix-v285.json . The 104 alternative GH/RD-only cards have no Dexa-bound runtime schema and are not model choices. Five operator mirror CF tools are indexed in the operator-only appendix but never made Dexa planner capabilities.

A multi-provider dependent workflow without a server-side audited identity/data adapter remains outside the one-tool minimal-intent contract; never invent a provider result shape or model-authored JSON path.

## B. 282 external NON-ANDROID catalog cards (full retained values)

The following array retains all non-Android external cards; legacy CF Android duplicates are retired in favor of Dexa native kernel, and MUST NOT be planned. Each entry retains complete Persian/English descriptions, ID, provider, risk, physical routes and original position.

## D. All 178 NON-ANDROID CF tool schemas and metadata

Native schemas define legal input arguments, not permission to execute. All non-Android original schemas are retained; overlapping CF Android schemas have been retired because native Android has one authoritative kernel.
```json
{"schema":"dexa.connector-schemas.v1","cf":{"cf_auth_status":{"readOnly":true,"description":"Return this caller's anonymized owner fingerprint and current owner-authorization mode. This tool is intentionally available even to an unauthorized caller so a new owner can obtain a fingerprint for an existing owner to approve.","inputSchema":{"$schema":"http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#","type":"object","properties":{}}},"cf_whoami":{"readOnly":true,"description":"Read-only gate-exempt caller identity diagnostic. Only an authorized owner may call this. The final owner cannot be removed through MCP.","inputSchema":{"type":"object","properties":{"subject_fingerprint":{"type":"string","pattern":"^cfsub_[0-9a-f]{64}$"}},"required":["subject_fingerprint"],"additionalProperties":false,"$schema":"http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#"}},"cf_auth_mode_set":{"readOnly":false,"description":"Set owner authorization mode to disabled, observe, or enforce. Only an authorized owner may call this. Enforcement requires at least one owner.","inputSchema":{"type":"object","properties":{"mode":{"type":"string","enum":["disabled","observe","enforce"]}},"required":["mode"],"additionalProperties":false,"$schema":"http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#"}},"cf_oauth_approve":{"readOnly":false,"description":"Approve a one-time OAuth pairing code from the authorization screen. Authorized ChatGPT owners only.","inputSchema":{"type":"object","properties":{"code":{"type":"string","pattern":"^CF-[0-9A-F]{12}$"}},"required":["code"],"additionalProperties":false,"$schema":"http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#"}},"onshape_operation_execute":{"readOnly":false,"description":"Use this semantic intent tool for ordinary human Onshape commands. Do not guess operationIds and do not perform source/OpenAPI discovery.\nAny change inside a document (add, edit, suppress, delete feature, delete part, delete element) is an ordinary reversible write: never ask the owner to confirm it and never set owner_confirmed_high_impact for it. Only sharing, permissions, ownership, API keys, webhooks and billing need confirmation, and the tool itself says so with ONSHAPE_HIGH_IMPACT_CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED.\ndocumented.updateDocumentAttributes: intents=[Update document attributes by document ID. | rename document | update document metadata]; target=documented operation path parameters; common document/workspace/element/entity ids auto-map from target; args=query/body/headers/multipart follow the precompiled documented contract; backend=DOCUMENTED_OPERATION\ndocumented.updateWVEPMetadata: intents=[Update the metadata for a part. | update metadata | update part metadata]; target=documented operation path parameters; common document/workspace/element/entity ids auto-map from target; args=query/body/headers/multipart follow the precompiled documented contract; backend=DOCUMENTED_OPERATION\ndocumented.createPartStudio: intents=[Create a new Part Studio in a document. | create part studio | add part studio | new part studio]; target=documented operation path parameters; common document/workspace/element/entity ids auto-map from target; args=query/body/headers/multipart follow the precompiled documented contract; backend=DOCUMENTED_OPERATION\ndocumented.updatePartStudioFeature: intents=[Update the definition of a Part Studio feature. | update feature | edit feature | update part studio feature]; target=documented operation path parameters; common document/workspace/element/entity ids auto-map from target; args=query/body/headers/multipart follow the precompiled documented contract; backend=DOCUMENTED_OPERATION\ndocumented.deletePartStudioFeature: intents=[Delete a Part Studio feature. | delete feature | remove feature | delete part studio feature]; target=documented operation path parameters; common document/workspace/element/entity ids auto-map from target; args=query/body/headers/multipart follow the precompiled documented contract; backend=DOCUMENTED_OPERATION\ndocumented.addPartStudioFeature: intents=[Add a feature to the Part Studio's Feature List. | add feature | create feature | add part studio feature]; target=documented operation path parameters; common document/workspace/element/entity ids auto-map from target; args=query/body/headers/multipart follow the precompiled documented contract; backend=DOCUMENTED_OPERATION\nviewer.inspect: intents=[inspect viewer | probe viewer | read viewer selection]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=mode:\"state\"|\"selection\"|\"probe\"|\"methods\"|\"method_details\"|\"selection_scan\"|\"collaboration\"; collaboration inspects only the persistent work page and never creates participant tabs; selection_scan returns bounded Face/Edge display-entity metadata from fixed module 45867 exports; probe requires x_fraction and y_fraction from 0 to 1; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nviewer.hover_probe: intents=[highlight viewer target | hover viewer target]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=x_fraction and y_fraction from 0 to 1, or world_point:{x,y,z} projected against the same live Viewer camera; optional expected_deterministic_id enforces an exact match. Exact world-point targets may dispatch one source-qualified Viewer.animateZoomFit(false) full-document fit on the same ephemeral page after an exact pre-pick miss, then retry with fresh projection; remaining pre-effect readiness misses stay bounded. UI picks verify through getHoveredSelection; model picks require exact pre-pick identity, exact ModelSelection identity, a live-qualified Viewer.doPick contract, and doPick=true from the model-selection handler. Same-pixel post-pick remains diagnostic only.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nviewer.selection: intents=[select viewer target | select entity | add to selection | clear viewer selection | deselect entity]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=action:select|add|clear. select replaces the current selection; add Shift-clicks one additional deterministic target while preserving prior exact identities; both require x_fraction/y_fraction from 0 to 1 and may include expected_deterministic_id. clear removes the persistent Viewer selection.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nview.move: intents=[move view | pan view | orbit view | zoom view]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=action:pan|orbit|zoom; direction pan=left|right|up|down in user screen-space, orbit=left|right|up|down|clockwise|counterclockwise, zoom=in|out; intensity:number in (0,5] with nonlinear scaling. Orbit may alternatively include angle_degrees in (0,360]. Values <=1 preserve fine-control compatibility; values >1 provide progressively larger movement.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nview.fit: intents=[fit view | fit all | fit selection | zoom to selection]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=action:fit|fit_selection. fit uses the native Viewer fit bounds on the persistent work page. fit_selection consumes the existing persistent Viewer selection and preserves its exact deterministic identities.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nview.standard: intents=[standard view | top view | show top view]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=view:top. Uses the fixed Onshape Shift+5 standard-view shortcut on the persistent work page and verifies the resulting data-view-shown tag.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nview.follow: intents=[follow collaborator | follow my view | follow view]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=optional candidate_index. With exactly two collaborator icons the live-qualified remote candidate index 1 is selected automatically; with three or more collaborators candidate_index is required to avoid guessing.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nview.follow_test: intents=[test follow handoff | test onshape follow]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=page_count:2|3. Two-page mode qualifies follow -> local break -> reverse follow using only the fixed collaborator icon path and camera convergence. Three-page mode records bounded collaborator DOM/disambiguation evidence.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nviewer.remote_ground_test: intents=[test remote selection grounding | test selection grounding]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=No arguments. Bounded qualification only: establishes Follow to an ephemeral leader, marks one deterministic geometry target on the leader, re-picks the same screen point locally on the persistent follower, and verifies deterministic identity.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nfeature.reorder_probe: intents=[probe feature reorder machinery | inspect feature reorder machinery]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=No arguments. Read-only bounded qualification probe over Feature-list DOM, attached drag/drop/pointer listeners, and loaded Onshape webpack factories matching reorder-specific terms. No generic JavaScript surface.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nfeature.reorder: intents=[move feature | reorder feature]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=source_feature_id or source_feature_name; target_feature_id or target_feature_name; placement:before|after. Exact feature identities are resolved through getPartStudioFeatures before the bounded drag, then order is verified by fresh feature-list readback.; backend=BOUNDED_UI\npart.visibility: intents=[hide part | show part | set part visibility]; target=document_id, workspace_id, element_id, plus part_id/entity_id or part_name/entity_name; args=visible:boolean; hide/show aliases supply visible automatically; backend=BOUNDED_UI\nfeature.delete_part: intents=[delete part | remove part | delete body | حذف پارت]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id, plus part_name or part_id; args=none; the admitted deleteBodies payload is derived from the resolved part query; backend=DERIVED_PAYLOAD\nfeature.parameter.set: intents=[set feature parameter | change fillet radius | set feature dimension]; target=document_url or ids, plus feature_name or feature_id; args=parameter_id or parameter_name, plus expression (for example \"5 mm\"), value for boolean/enum parameters, or part_name/part_names to replace the parts a query parameter points at; backend=DERIVED_PAYLOAD\nfeature.delete: intents=[delete feature | delete last feature | حذف فیچر]; target=document_url or ids; feature_name or feature_id, otherwise the last feature is used; args=position:\"last\"|\"first\" when no feature is named; backend=DERIVED_PAYLOAD\nfeature.add: intents=[add feature | add plane | linear pattern | add fillet]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id; args=feature_type (validated against this Part Studio's feature specs), optional name, and parameters as an object of parameterId to value; quantities take strings such as \"30 mm\", geometry references take part names, feature names, or Top/Front/Right; an empty list [] is allowed and the feature is committed even if it regenerates with an error (reported as featureStatus); built from the official feature spec, no existing feature needed; backend=DERIVED_PAYLOAD\nfeature.from_selection: intents=[feature from current selection | fillet current selection | chamfer current selection]; target=document_url or document_id/workspace_id/element_id with an existing persistent Viewer selection; args=feature_type plus non-selection feature parameters. The existing exact deterministic Viewer selection is first-class input and is bridged to documented BTMIndividualQuery deterministicIds without re-picking coordinates. Optional selection_parameter_id disambiguates features with multiple query parameters. qualification_route:auto|api|ui is admitted only for bounded qualification/benchmarking of the internal route; ordinary use should omit it and remain implementation-independent.; backend=DERIVED_PAYLOAD\nfeature.patch: intents=[edit feature | suppress feature | rename feature]; target=document_url or ids, plus feature_name or feature_id (otherwise the last feature); args=any combination of suppressed:boolean, new_name, and parameters as an object of parameterId to expression, value, or geometry reference list; backend=DERIVED_PAYLOAD\nrollback.set: intents=[set rollback | move rollback bar]; target=document_url or ids; args=before_feature or after_feature by name, or position:\"end\"|\"start\", or rollback_index (-1 means end); backend=DERIVED_PAYLOAD\nmetadata.property.set: intents=[set part color | set metadata property | رنگ پارت]; target=document_url or ids, plus part_name/part_id for a part property; without a part the element property is set; args=property_name or property_id plus value; or color (name, #rrggbb, or {red,green,blue}) for the Appearance property; backend=DERIVED_PAYLOAD\nExact documented operationIds and their precompiled summaries are also accepted as intents for advanced documented ## F. Final reminder
```
For every new request return exactly one complete JSON response: either an executable dexa.tool-plan.v3 recipe for Rana, or {"schema":"dexa.tool-plan.v3","status":"ANSWER","reply":"Complete answer or clarification question"}. Do not emit NEEDS_INPUT, UNRESOLVED, USER_GATE or an unfinished mission for a conversational answer. Never claim a tool executed unless Rana has returned observed results. Previously completed owner–Dexa exchanges, when appended below, are context for understanding the next independent request, not a way to resume the old mission.

## DEXA ANDROID NATIVE KERNEL — 47 TYPED TOOL CONTRACTS (v1)

Authoritative INTERNAL Rana binding, NOT a first-model prompt and NOT a grant of Android permissions or a transcript. These contracts come from Java native handlers and the same server-side allowlist used by Rana.
Model: choose `dexa.android.invoke` with `dexa.intent.v1` semantic args only. Rana compiles the mechanical `dexa.tool-plan.v3` steps and dataflow itself. Exact args: `{"action":"<ID below>","parameters":{...}}` and optional `deviceId` (only if observed in an authenticated device inventory). `parameters` MUST conform to that specific action's `parametersSchema` (exact field names, required keys, enum, type, limits, and no extra keys). `requires` and `usageNotes` are binding operating guidance. Read live `capability.inventory` or `device.state.get` when availability matters; this static catalog NEVER proves permissions are granted.
For `dexa.android.status` use only an observed `{request_id:UUID}`; Rana handles asynchronous polling and signed receipt correlation. Do NOT insert a separate status step in a model plan. QUEUED and DELIVERED are NOT success. Legacy v44 WAITING_USER_CONFIRMATION is also NOT success; v45 never creates it. Never claim final Android side effects from INITIATED or MEDIUM/WEAK evidence; inspect target state where feasible. R3 denotes risk, not an extra prompt. Android permissions remain required. Do not ask for duplicate Dexa confirmation or a confirm=true flag. No invented tools, raw shell, JavaScript, coordinate taps or privilege bypass.
Example READ args (only after permission preflight): `{"action":"sms.list","parameters":{"box":"inbox","limit":1}}`. Example setting request: `{"action":"settings.open","parameters":{"section":"wifi"}}`; this OPENS Settings and does NOT toggle Wi-Fi. For SMS or destructive actions obtain exact observed recipient, message or notification identifiers; NEVER substitute a documentation example. Prefer minimum sufficient read bounds, do not guess unsupported `app.install` or `sms.forward` (compose supported read/send tools instead).
The 47 records below define descriptionEn/descriptionFa, schema, prerequisites, operational caveats, expected result fields and native receipt interpretation in one parseable list.
